DESIGN.md

show
## Concept
The player taps to toggle a dot between a tiny and a large radius, threading through narrow gaps when small and smashing weak barriers or reaching distant platforms when large. Rapid, rhythmic size changes create a satisfying tension‑and‑release loop.

## Player
A glowing dot that can be tiny or large. Verbs: **move** (directional input), **toggle size** (tap or key press). The avatar is a simple luminous sphere, visually minimal.

## Core loop
Every few seconds the player encounters a new segment of obstacles. They read the shape of the upcoming gap or barrier, decide whether to stay small to slip through or grow large to break through, then toggle size at the right moment while moving forward. Success rewards a visual burst and a point; failure ends the run. The loop repeats, increasing the frequency and variety of size‑decision moments.

## Hazards / Obstacles
- **Narrow gaps**: static walls with tight openings that only a tiny dot can pass.
- **Fragile barriers**: blocks that shatter when the player is large, disappearing after a smash.
- **Moving spikes**: horizontal or vertical sweepers that punish mistimed size changes.
- **Falling shards**: projectiles that rain down; they can be avoided by staying small or destroyed by being large.
- **Timed platforms**: floating platforms that appear only when the player is large, allowing jumps to higher sections.

## Score
- **Gap slip**: each successful pass through a narrow gap adds a point.
- **Barrier smash**: each shattered fragile barrier adds a point.
- **Distance**: traveling a set segment distance awards a bonus.
- **Combo**: consecutive correct size toggles without a miss grant extra points.

## Win and lose
- **Win**: Reach the end of the level (the final platform) and trigger a "You Win" burst.
- **Lose**: Collide with a barrier while the dot is the wrong size, or be hit by a moving spike. The screen flashes, displays "Game Over" with the final score, and shows restart instructions.

## Feel
Frantic, pulse‑driven gameplay with a neon‑lit, cyber‑punk aesthetic. Dark background contrasted by bright cyan and magenta glows. Tight, syncopated chiptune beats accent each toggle, with sharp sound effects for smashing and slipping.

## Why it's fun
The instant joy of perfectly timing a size change to zip through an impossible gap or crush an obstacle makes the player smile, curse, and shout for one more try.

TASKS.md

show
1. Scaffold HTML and meta.json — create `index.html` with required header, canvas, restart button, empty script block, and generate `meta.json` matching title, description, controls, tags.
2. Define game state objects — add plain object literals for player (position, velocity, radii, current radius), hazards array, score, state, objective.
3. Implement animation loop — write `startLoop` with `cancelAnimationFrame`, dt calculation, try/catch, and canvas clearing each frame.
4. Set up input handling — create `keys` Set, keydown/keyup listeners, map R key and restart button to a shared `reset()` function.
5. Add player movement — dt‑based physics updating player position based on arrow/WASD keys, clamped within canvas bounds.
6. Implement size toggle — handle tap/Space key to switch player radius between small and large values.
7. Create hazard system — define hazard data structure, spawn logic respecting `SAFE_RADIUS`, and per‑frame motion updates.
8. Add collision detection — detect player‑hazard collisions, transition to "gameover" state, and stop active gameplay.
9. Implement scoring — increase score on successful hazard passes or distance milestones, and draw the score on canvas.
10. Render core visuals — clear canvas, draw background, hazards, player (high‑contrast), and HUD elements each frame.
11. Game‑over overlay — dim background, display "Game Over" with final score and restart instructions on canvas.
12. Expose observable contract — assign `window.__game` each frame with state, score, player position/visibility, and objective string.
13. Add audio effects — create lazy‑init `beep()` using `AudioContext`, trigger sounds on hit, score, and death events.

    canvas { background: #0f1320; border: 1px solid #2a2f44; }
    .game-restart { margin-top: 10px; padding: 8px 14px; background: #1c2030; color: #7df9ff; border: 1px solid #2a2f44; cursor: pointer; }
    .game-restart:hover { background: #232842; }
  </style>

<body>
  <header class="game-header">
    <h1>Size Shift</h1>
    <p class="game-description">Tap to toggle a glowing dot between tiny and large sizes, threading through narrow gaps when small and smashing weak barriers or reaching distant platforms when large. Rapid size changes create a tense, rhythmic challenge.</p>
    <ul class="game-controls">
      <li>Tap to toggle size</li>
      <li>Arrow keys or WASD to move</li>
      <li>Press R or click Restart</li>
    </ul>
  </header>
  <main>
    <canvas id="game" width="800" height="600"></canvas>
    <button id="restart" class="game-restart" type="button">Restart</button>
  </main>
  <script>
    // === CONFIG =============================================================
// (no configuration needed yet)

// === STATE ==============================================================
const canvas = document.getElementById("game");
const ctx = canvas.getContext("2d");

// Player object definition
const player = {
  x: canvas.width / 2,
  y: canvas.height / 2,
  vx: 0,
  vy: 0,
  radiusSmall: 10,
  radiusLarge: 30,
  radius: 10,
};

// Hazards array
let hazards = [];

// Score and game state
let score = 0;
let state = "playing"; // "playing" | "gameover"
let objective = "Toggle size to thread through narrow gaps and smash weak barriers.";

// === INPUT ==============================================================
let keys = new Set();

function reset() {
  // TODO: implement reset logic later
  console.log('reset called');
}

const restartBtn = document.getElementById('restart');
restartBtn.addEventListener('click', reset);

document.addEventListener('keydown', (ev) => {
  if (ev.key === 'r' || ev.key === 'R') {
    reset();
    return;
  }
  keys.add(ev.key);
});

document.addEventListener('keyup', (ev) => {
  keys.delete(ev.key);
});

// Size toggle handling: Space key
document.addEventListener('keydown', (ev) => {
  if (ev.key === ' ') {
    player.radius = (player.radius === player.radiusSmall) ? player.radiusLarge : player.radiusSmall;
  }
});

// Size toggle on canvas tap/click
canvas.addEventListener('click', () => {
  player.radius = (player.radius === player.radiusSmall) ? player.radiusLarge : player.radiusSmall;
});
// === HAZARD SYSTEM ==========================================================
const HAZARD_SPEED = 150; // px/sec
const SPAWN_INTERVAL = 1.5; // seconds
const SAFE_RADIUS = 200;

// Timer for spawning hazards
let spawnTimer = 0;

// Define a hazard as an object { x, y, radius, speed, alive }
function spawnHazard() {
  // spawn at right edge, random y
  const y = Math.random() * (canvas.height - 40) + 20;
  const hx = canvas.width + 20;
  // Ensure safe distance from player
  const dx = hx - player.x;
  const dy = y - player.y;
  if (Math.hypot(dx, dy) < SAFE_RADIUS) {
    // Too close, skip this spawn
    return;
  }
  hazards.push({ x: hx, y, radius: 12, speed: HAZARD_SPEED, alive: true });
}

function updateHazards(dt) {
  for (let i = hazards.length - 1; i >= 0; i--) {
    const h = hazards[i];
    h.x -= h.speed * dt;
    if (h.x < -h.radius) {
      hazards.splice(i, 1);
      score += 10; // increase score when hazard passes
    }
  }
}

// Main update function placeholder (called each frame by startLoop)
function update(dt) {
  // Hazard spawning
  spawnTimer -= dt;
  if (spawnTimer <= 0) {
    spawnHazard();
    spawnTimer = SPAWN_INTERVAL;
  }
  // Update existing hazards
  updateHazards(dt);

  // Collision detection between player and hazards
  if (state === "playing") {
    for (const h of hazards) {
      const dx = h.x - player.x;
      const dy = h.y - player.y;
      const distSq = dx * dx + dy * dy;
      const radSum = h.radius + player.radius;
      if (distSq < radSum * radSum) {
        state = "gameover";
        // Optionally, could stop further updates or break
        break;
      }
    }
  }
}
// === OBSERVABLE CONTRACT ==================================================
function publishState() {
  window.__game = {
    state: state,
    score: score,
    player: {
      x: player.x,
      y: player.y,
      visible: player.x >= 0 && player.x <= canvas.width && player.y >= 0 && player.y <= canvas.height,
    },
    objective: objective,
  };
}

// Publish initial observable state
publishState();

  </script>
</body>
</html>

On restart you MUST `cancelAnimationFrame` any pending loop before starting a new one (otherwise loops stack on each restart and the game eventually slows to a crawl).\n95: 5. **Progressive difficulty** — start gentle so the player can learn, then ramp up. Don't dump them into chaos at t=0.\n96: 6. **No dead time** — something interesting on screen at all times. Idle background animation (stars, gradient pulse, particles) is fine.\n97: 7. **Persistent score / progress UI** — drawn on the canvas at the top corner, always visible.\n98: 8. **Audio (optional but encouraged)** — generate tones with `OscillatorNode` (no external assets). **Do not auto-start audio on page load** — browsers block this and it will throw. Initialize the `AudioContext` lazily on the first keypress or click. Wrap audio code in try/catch.\n99: \n100: ## Playable Contract — non-negotiable\n101: \n102: These are not aspirations. A game that violates any of these is broken even if it runs without errors. After your implementation finishes, the validator loads the page in a real browser and runs an 8-second playtest with synthetic input — failures here are reported back to you and must be fixed before the game ships.\n103: \n104: ### 1. The player can lose\n105: \n106: There must be a collision check (or other end condition) that fires `gameOver` and stops the active gameplay loop. \"Game runs forever, no death\" is broken. Examples that have failed in the past: collision check compared a value against itself; \"spike\" collision only clamped position instead of killing the player; obstacles never reached the player.\n107: \n108: ### 2. The player can score\n109: \n110: The score must increase on a **discrete event** (a survived obstacle, a collected pickup, an enemy destroyed, a beat hit). \"Score = elapsed seconds\" is allowed but weak. Score must be drawn on the canvas at all times.\n111: \n112: ### 3. The first 3 seconds are survivable with no input\n113: \n114: Define a `SAFE_RADIUS` (e.g. 200px) around the player's starting position. When spawning hazards, **reject any spawn that is within `SAFE_RADIUS` of the player AND would reach them within 3 seconds at current hazard speed**. Pre-spawning hazards on top of the player at t=0 has caused frame-1 deaths in past games. Don't do it.\n115: \n116: ### 4. The control actually changes the outcome\n117: \n118: Whatever the player presses must, in the next frame, change a value that affects collision or score. If a control is purely cosmetic (e.g. \"stretch horizontally\" but the gaps are vertical), the game is fundamentally broken even if it runs. After writing, **re-read the game's concept and trace one frame**: does the input do what the concept claims?\n119: \n120: ### 5. dt-based physics, not per-frame constants\n121: \n122: All movement and timing must scale by delta time (seconds since last frame). Per-frame constants (`x += 5` every frame) make games run 2.4× faster on a 144Hz display.\n123: \n124: ```js\n125: let lastTime = performance.now();\n126: function loop(now) {\n127:   const dt = Math.min((now - lastTime) / 1000, 0.1); // seconds, clamped\n128:   lastTime = now;\n129:   // physics scaled by dt:\n130:   player.x += player.vx * dt;\n131:   enemy.y  += enemy.vy  * dt;\n132:   spawnTimer -= dt;\n133:   // ...\n134:   requestAnimationFrame(loop);\n135: }\n136: requestAnimationFrame(loop);\n137: ```\n138: \n139: ### 6. Restart fully resets AND cancels the prior loop\n140: \n141: Both R and the Restart button must call the same reset function. That function MUST `cancelAnimationFrame(loopId)` the previous loop before scheduling a new one — otherwise loops stack on each restart and the game slows to a crawl over time.\n142: \n143: ```js\n144: let loopId = null;\n145: function startLoop() {\n146:   if (loopId !== null) cancelAnimationFrame(loopId);\n147:   function tick(now) { /* ... */ loopId = requestAnimationFrame(tick); }\n148:   loopId = requestAnimationFrame(tick);\n149: }\n150: ```\n151: \n152: ### 7. Game-over overlay shows score and restart instructions\n153: \n154: When the player dies, draw on the canvas: \"Game Over\", final score, current high score (if you persist one), and \"Press R or click Restart\". Do not rely on the HTML restart button alone — players need to know they can restart.\n155: \n156: ## Observable State Contract — required for playtest\n157: \n158: In addition to the rules above, your game **must** expose its current state on `window.__game` so the automated playtest can observe what the player would observe. A game that does not expose this object is rejected.\n159: \n160: ```js\n161: window.__game = {\n162:   state: \"menu\",        // \"menu\" | \"playing\" | \"gameover\" — exact strings, no others\n163:   score: 0,             // current score, number\n164:   player: {\n165:     x: 400,             // player x in canvas coordinates (0 = left)\n166:     y: 300,             // player y in canvas coordinates (0 = top)\n167:     visible: true,      // true if the player is currently rendered inside the canvas viewport\n168:   },\n169:   objective: \"Dodge falling neon blocks. Survive as long as possible.\", // 10-200 chars, one sentence\n170: };\n171: ```\n172: \n173: Rules:\n174: - The object must exist from the very first frame, before any input.\n175: - Update `state`, `score`, `player.x`, `player.y`, and `player.visible` whenever they change. The simplest approach is to update them at the end of your render/update step each frame.\n176: - `state` must only ever be one of the three exact strings. No other values.\n177: - `player.visible` is critical. **If your camera follows the player, the camera math must keep `player.visible` = true at all times.** A camera that lags the player off-screen is the most common failure here.\n178: - `objective` is one short sentence written for a first-time player: \"what am I trying to do?\" — derivable from observing the game alone, not from reading the meta.\n179: \n180: If your game has multiple \"players\" or no clear single player, expose the position of the most player-like entity (the thing the human controls).\n181: \n182: ## What the validator checks\n183: \n184: Your output is checked programmatically. Failure feeds the error report back to you for correction.\n185: \n186: 1. Both `index.html` and `meta.json` exist.\n187: 2. `meta.json` parses as JSON and matches the schema above.\n188: 3. `index.html` parses, has exactly one `<canvas id=\"game\">`, exactly one `<button id=\"restart\">` after it, and the `<header>` text matches `meta.json`.\n189: 4. No external URLs in `<script src=>`, `<link href=>`, `<img src=>` (regex scan).\n190: 5. Headless Chromium loads the page; no `console.error` or `pageerror` events in 5 seconds.\n191: 6. Canvas has non-blank pixels after 3 seconds (game is actually drawing something).\n192: 7. **Observable contract is present**: `window.__game` exists with the exact shape above, from the first frame.\n193: 8. **Playtest invariants** hold during ~8 seconds of synthetic key input (arrow keys, WASD, space):\n194:    - `state` only takes the values `\"menu\" | \"playing\" | \"gameover\"`.\n195:    - `player.visible` is true for ≥95% of polled frames.\n196:    - Either `score` changes at least once during play, or `state` reaches `\"gameover\"`. ```
function render() {
  ctx.clearRect(0,0,canvas.width,canvas.height);
  // draw player
  ctx.fillStyle = "#7df9ff";
  ctx.beginPath();
  ctx.arc(player.x, player.y, player.radius, 0, Math.PI*2);
  ctx.fill();
  // draw hazards
  ctx.fillStyle = "#ff5555";
  for (const h of hazards) {
    ctx.beginPath();
    ctx.arc(h.x, h.y, h.radius, 0, Math.PI*2);
    ctx.fill();
  }
}

// Game loop
let lastTime;
function startLoop(time) {
  if (!lastTime) {
    lastTime = time;
  }
  const dt = (time - lastTime) / 1000;
  lastTime = time;
  update(dt);
  render();
  publishState();
  if (state !== "gameover") {
    requestAnimationFrame(startLoop);
  }
}
requestAnimationFrame(startLoop);
```

This ensures game loop runs until gameover then stops.

Also may want to ensure canvas is ready: maybe run after DOM ready; script inside body after canvas element, so fine.

    // clear background
    ctx.fillStyle = "#0f1320";
    ctx.fillRect(0, 0, canvas.width, canvas.height);
    // draw player
    ctx.fillStyle = "#7df9ff";
    ctx.beginPath();

    ctx.fill();

        ctx.beginPath();

}

prompt (13,924 chars)
## Reference example: "breakout"

The following is a complete, validator-passing game. **Use it as a structural template** — your output must follow the same section ordering, the same patterns for the loop / restart / observable contract, and the same overall code style. Do NOT reproduce this game; you are building a different game described in the prompt below. The example exists only to anchor structure.

Notice in particular:
- The `// === SECTION ===` divider banners and their fixed order: CONFIG → STATE → RESET → LOOP/UPDATE → RENDER → INPUT → AUDIO → OBSERVABLE CONTRACT → BOOT.
- Plain object literals for game state (no `class`).
- A `keys` Set polled each frame instead of mutating game state from key handlers.
- `if (loopId !== null) cancelAnimationFrame(loopId)` at the top of `startLoop()`.
- The animation loop body wrapped in `try/catch`.
- `publishState()` called once per frame at the end of the tick.
- `window.__game` populated honestly (player.visible reflects actual viewport position).
- AudioContext initialized lazily inside `beep()`, wrapped in try/catch.

### Example `meta.json`
```json
{
  "title": "Neon Breaker",
  "description": "Bounce the ball with the paddle and clear every brick. One ball, no second chances.",
  "controls": [
    "Arrow keys or A/D to move the paddle",
    "Hold Space to launch faster",
    "Press R or click Restart"
  ],
  "tags": ["arcade", "breakout", "bouncing"]
}
```

### Example `index.html`
```html
<!DOCTYPE html>
<html lang="en">
<head>
  <meta charset="utf-8">
  <title>Neon Breaker</title>
  <style>
    body { margin: 0; background: #0a0a0f; color: #e6e6f0; font-family: ui-sans-serif, system-ui, sans-serif; }
    .game-header { padding: 12px 16px; border-bottom: 1px solid #1d1d2a; }
    .game-header h1 { margin: 0; font-size: 18px; color: #7df9ff; }
    .game-header .game-description { margin: 4px 0; color: #a5a5b8; }
    .game-header .game-controls { margin: 4px 0; padding-left: 18px; color: #c8c8d8; }
    main { display: flex; flex-direction: column; align-items: center; padding: 16px; }
    canvas { background: #0f1320; border: 1px solid #2a2f44; }
    .game-restart { margin-top: 10px; padding: 8px 14px; background: #1c2030; color: #7df9ff; border: 1px solid #2a2f44; cursor: pointer; }
    .game-restart:hover { background: #232842; }
  </style>
</head>
<body>
  <header class="game-header">
    <h1>Neon Breaker</h1>
    <p class="game-description">Bounce the ball with the paddle and clear every brick. One ball, no second chances.</p>
    <ul class="game-controls">
      <li>Arrow keys or A/D to move the paddle</li>
      <li>Hold Space to launch faster</li>
      <li>Press R or click Restart</li>
    </ul>
  </header>
  <main>
    <canvas id="game" width="800" height="600"></canvas>
    <button id="restart" class="game-restart" type="button">Restart</button>
  </main>
  <script>
  // === CONFIG =============================================================
  const PADDLE_W = 110;
  const PADDLE_H = 14;
  const PADDLE_Y = 560;
  const PADDLE_SPEED = 700;       // px/sec

  const BALL_R = 7;
  const BALL_BASE_SPEED = 360;    // px/sec at start
  const BALL_MAX_SPEED  = 720;
  const BALL_SPEEDUP_PER_HIT = 6; // px/sec added per brick

  const BRICK_ROWS = 6;
  const BRICK_COLS = 12;
  const BRICK_W = 60;
  const BRICK_H = 20;
  const BRICK_GAP = 4;
  const BRICK_TOP = 60;
  const BRICK_LEFT = (800 - (BRICK_COLS * (BRICK_W + BRICK_GAP) - BRICK_GAP)) / 2;
  const ROW_COLORS = ["#ff52ae", "#ff8c52", "#ffd23f", "#a3ff52", "#52ffae", "#7df9ff"];

  // === STATE ==============================================================
  const canvas = document.getElementById("game");
  const ctx = canvas.getContext("2d");
  const restartBtn = document.getElementById("restart");

  let paddle;        // { x } center x
  let ball;          // { x, y, vx, vy, speed }
  let bricks;        // array of { x, y, w, h, color, alive }
  let score;
  let bricksLeft;
  let phase;         // "playing" | "won" | "lost"
  let keys;          // pressed key set
  let highScore = 0;
  let lastTime;
  let loopId = null;
  let audioCtx = null;

  function reset() {
    paddle = { x: 400 };
    ball = { x: 400, y: 480, vx: 0, vy: 0, speed: BALL_BASE_SPEED };
    launchBall();
    bricks = [];
    for (let r = 0; r < BRICK_ROWS; r++) {
      for (let c = 0; c < BRICK_COLS; c++) {
        bricks.push({
          x: BRICK_LEFT + c * (BRICK_W + BRICK_GAP),
          y: BRICK_TOP + r * (BRICK_H + BRICK_GAP),
          w: BRICK_W, h: BRICK_H,
          color: ROW_COLORS[r % ROW_COLORS.length],
          alive: true,
        });
      }
    }
    bricksLeft = bricks.length;
    score = 0;
    phase = "playing";
    keys = new Set();
    lastTime = performance.now();
    publishState();
    startLoop();
  }

  function launchBall() {
    // Always launch upward, mild horizontal lean either side.
    const angle = (-Math.PI / 2) + (Math.random() * 0.6 - 0.3);
    ball.vx = Math.cos(angle) * ball.speed;
    ball.vy = Math.sin(angle) * ball.speed;
  }

  function startLoop() {
    if (loopId !== null) cancelAnimationFrame(loopId);
    function tick(now) {
      try {
        const dt = Math.min((now - lastTime) / 1000, 0.1);
        lastTime = now;
        update(dt);
        render();
        publishState();
      } catch (err) {
        console.error("loop error", err);
      }
      loopId = requestAnimationFrame(tick);
    }
    loopId = requestAnimationFrame(tick);
  }

  // === UPDATE =============================================================
  function update(dt) {
    if (phase !== "playing") return;

    // Paddle.
    const dirX = (keys.has("ArrowRight") || keys.has("d") || keys.has("D") ? 1 : 0)
               - (keys.has("ArrowLeft")  || keys.has("a") || keys.has("A") ? 1 : 0);
    const speedMul = keys.has(" ") ? 1.6 : 1;
    paddle.x += dirX * PADDLE_SPEED * speedMul * dt;
    paddle.x = clamp(paddle.x, PADDLE_W / 2, canvas.width - PADDLE_W / 2);

    // Ball motion.
    ball.x += ball.vx * dt;
    ball.y += ball.vy * dt;

    // Walls.
    if (ball.x < BALL_R) { ball.x = BALL_R; ball.vx = Math.abs(ball.vx); beep(220, 0.04); }
    if (ball.x > canvas.width - BALL_R) { ball.x = canvas.width - BALL_R; ball.vx = -Math.abs(ball.vx); beep(220, 0.04); }
    if (ball.y < BALL_R) { ball.y = BALL_R; ball.vy = Math.abs(ball.vy); beep(220, 0.04); }

    // Floor — lose.
    if (ball.y > canvas.height + BALL_R) {
      phase = "lost";
      if (score > highScore) highScore = score;
      beep(110, 0.25);
      return;
    }

    // Paddle.
    const px = paddle.x, py = PADDLE_Y;
    if (ball.vy > 0
        && ball.y + BALL_R >= py
        && ball.y - BALL_R <= py + PADDLE_H
        && ball.x >= px - PADDLE_W / 2 - BALL_R
        && ball.x <= px + PADDLE_W / 2 + BALL_R) {
      // Bounce direction depends on where it hit on the paddle.
      const offset = (ball.x - px) / (PADDLE_W / 2); // -1..1
      const angle = (-Math.PI / 2) + offset * 1.0;   // up-ish, leaning side
      ball.vx = Math.cos(angle) * ball.speed;
      ball.vy = Math.sin(angle) * ball.speed;
      ball.y = py - BALL_R - 0.1;
      beep(540, 0.05);
    }

    // Bricks.
    for (const b of bricks) {
      if (!b.alive) continue;
      if (ball.x + BALL_R < b.x || ball.x - BALL_R > b.x + b.w) continue;
      if (ball.y + BALL_R < b.y || ball.y - BALL_R > b.y + b.h) continue;

      b.alive = false;
      bricksLeft--;
      score += 10;
      ball.speed = Math.min(BALL_MAX_SPEED, ball.speed + BALL_SPEEDUP_PER_HIT);

      // Decide which side was hit by comparing penetrations.
      const overlapX = Math.min(ball.x + BALL_R - b.x, b.x + b.w - (ball.x - BALL_R));
      const overlapY = Math.min(ball.y + BALL_R - b.y, b.y + b.h - (ball.y - BALL_R));
      if (overlapX < overlapY) ball.vx = -ball.vx; else ball.vy = -ball.vy;

      // Renormalize to current speed.
      const m = Math.hypot(ball.vx, ball.vy);
      ball.vx = (ball.vx / m) * ball.speed;
      ball.vy = (ball.vy / m) * ball.speed;
      beep(880, 0.05);
      break;
    }

    if (bricksLeft === 0) {
      phase = "won";
      if (score > highScore) highScore = score;
      beep(990, 0.2);
    }
  }

  // === RENDER =============================================================
  function render() {
    ctx.fillStyle = "#0f1320";
    ctx.fillRect(0, 0, canvas.width, canvas.height);

    // Bricks.
    for (const b of bricks) {
      if (!b.alive) continue;
      ctx.fillStyle = b.color;
      ctx.fillRect(b.x, b.y, b.w, b.h);
    }

    // Paddle.
    ctx.fillStyle = "#7df9ff";
    ctx.fillRect(paddle.x - PADDLE_W / 2, PADDLE_Y, PADDLE_W, PADDLE_H);

    // Ball.
    ctx.fillStyle = "#ffffff";
    ctx.beginPath();
    ctx.arc(ball.x, ball.y, BALL_R, 0, Math.PI * 2);
    ctx.fill();

    // HUD.
    ctx.fillStyle = "#e6e6f0";
    ctx.font = "16px ui-sans-serif, system-ui";
    ctx.textAlign = "left";
    ctx.fillText(`Score ${score}`, 12, 22);
    ctx.textAlign = "right";
    ctx.fillText(`Bricks ${bricksLeft}  ·  Best ${highScore}`, canvas.width - 12, 22);

    if (phase === "lost" || phase === "won") {
      ctx.fillStyle = "rgba(10,10,15,0.7)";
      ctx.fillRect(0, 0, canvas.width, canvas.height);
      ctx.textAlign = "center";
      ctx.font = "44px ui-sans-serif, system-ui";
      ctx.fillStyle = phase === "won" ? "#52ffae" : "#ff52ae";
      ctx.fillText(phase === "won" ? "You Win!" : "Game Over", canvas.width / 2, canvas.height / 2 - 20);
      ctx.fillStyle = "#e6e6f0";
      ctx.font = "20px ui-sans-serif, system-ui";
      ctx.fillText(`Final score ${score}`, canvas.width / 2, canvas.height / 2 + 14);
      ctx.fillStyle = "#a5a5b8";
      ctx.font = "16px ui-sans-serif, system-ui";
      ctx.fillText("Press R or click Restart", canvas.width / 2, canvas.height / 2 + 42);
    }
  }

  // === INPUT ==============================================================
  document.addEventListener("keydown", (ev) => {
    if (ev.key === "r" || ev.key === "R") { reset(); return; }
    keys.add(ev.key);
  });
  document.addEventListener("keyup", (ev) => { keys.delete(ev.key); });
  restartBtn.addEventListener("click", reset);

  // === AUDIO (lazy) =======================================================
  function beep(freq, durSec) {
    try {
      if (!audioCtx) audioCtx = new (window.AudioContext || window.webkitAudioContext)();
      const osc = audioCtx.createOscillator();
      const gain = audioCtx.createGain();
      osc.type = "square";
      osc.frequency.value = freq;
      gain.gain.setValueAtTime(0.05, audioCtx.currentTime);
      gain.gain.exponentialRampToValueAtTime(0.001, audioCtx.currentTime + durSec);
      osc.connect(gain); gain.connect(audioCtx.destination);
      osc.start(); osc.stop(audioCtx.currentTime + durSec);
    } catch {}
  }

  // === OBSERVABLE CONTRACT ===============================================
  function publishState() {
    window.__game = {
      state: phase === "playing" ? "playing" : "gameover",
      score,
      player: {
        x: paddle.x,
        y: PADDLE_Y + PADDLE_H / 2,
        visible: paddle.x >= 0 && paddle.x <= canvas.width,
      },
      objective: "Bounce the ball with the paddle to break every brick.",
    };
  }

  // === HELPERS ============================================================
  function clamp(v, lo, hi) { return v < lo ? lo : (v > hi ? hi : v); }

  // === BOOT ===============================================================
  reset();
  </script>
</body>
</html>
```

The player can score\n109: \n110: The score must increase on a **discrete event** (a survived obstacle, a collected pickup, an enemy destroyed, a beat hit). \"Score = elapsed seconds\" is allowed but weak. The first 3 seconds are survivable with no input\n113: \n114: Define a `SAFE_RADIUS` (e.g. 200px) around the player's starting position. When spawning hazards, **reject any spawn that is within `SAFE_RADIUS` of the player AND would reach them within 3 seconds at current hazard speed**. Pre-spawning hazards on top of the player at t=0 has caused frame-1 deaths in past games. Don't do it.\n115: \n116: ### 4. The control actually changes the outcome\n117: \n118: Whatever the player presses must, in the next frame, change a value that affects collision or score. If a control is purely cosmetic (e.g. \"stretch horizontally\" but the gaps are vertical), the game is fundamentally broken even if it runs. After writing, **re-read the game's concept and trace one frame**: does the input do what the concept claims?\n119: \n120: ### 5. dt-based physics, not per-frame constants\n121: \n122: All movement and timing must scale by delta time (seconds since last frame). Per-frame constants (`x += 5` every frame) make games run 2.4× faster on a 144Hz display.\n123: \n124: ```js\n125: let lastTime = performance.now();\n126: function loop(now) {\n127:   const dt = Math.min((now - lastTime) / 1000, 0.1); // seconds, clamped\n128:   lastTime = now;\n129:   // physics scaled by dt:\n130:   player.x += player.vx * dt;\n131:   enemy.y  += enemy.vy  * dt;\n132:   spawnTimer -= dt;\n133:   // ...\n134:   requestAnimationFrame(loop);\n135: }\n136: requestAnimationFrame(loop);\n137: ```\n138: \n139: ### 6. Restart fully resets AND cancels the prior loop\n140: \n141: Both R and the Restart button must call the same reset function. That function MUST `cancelAnimationFrame(loopId)` the previous loop before scheduling a new one — otherwise loops stack on each restart and the game slows to a crawl over time.\n142: \n143: ```js\n144: let loopId = null;\n145: function startLoop() {\n146:   if (loopId !== null) cancelAnimationFrame(loopId);\n147:   function tick(now) { /* ... */ loopId = requestAnimationFrame(tick); }\n148:   loopId = requestAnimationFrame(tick);\n149: }\n150: ```\n151: \n152: ### 7. Game-over overlay shows score and restart instructions\n153: \n154: When the player dies, draw on the canvas: \"Game Over\", final score, current high score (if you persist one), and \"Press R or click Restart\". Do not rely on the HTML restart button alone — players need to know they can restart.\n155: \n156: ## Observable State Contract — required for playtest\n157: \n158: In addition to the rules above, your game **must** expose its current state on `window.__game` so the automated playtest can observe what the player would observe. A game that does not expose this object is rejected.\n159: \n160: ```js\n161: window.__game = {\n162:   state: \"menu\",        // \"menu\" | \"playing\" | \"gameover\" — exact strings, no others\n163:   score: 0,             // current score, number\n164:   player: {\n165:     x: 400,             // player x in canvas coordinates (0 = left)\n166:     y: 300,             // player y in canvas coordinates (0 = top)\n167:     visible: true,      // true if the player is currently rendered inside the canvas viewport\n168:   },\n169:   objective: \"Dodge falling neon blocks. Survive as long as possible.\", // 10-200 chars, one sentence\n170: };\n171: ```\n172: \n173: Rules:\n174: - The object must exist from the very first frame, before any input.\n175: - Update `state`, `score`, `player.x`, `player.y`, and `player.visible` whenever they change. The simplest approach is to update them at the end of your render/update step each frame.\n176: - `state` must only ever be one of the three exact strings. No other values.\n177: - `player.visible` is critical. **If your camera follows the player, the camera math must keep `player.visible` = true at all times.** A camera that lags the player off-screen is the most common failure here.\n178: - `objective` is one short sentence written for a first-time player: \"what am I trying to do?\" — derivable from observing the game alone, not from reading the meta.\n179: \n180: If your game has multiple \"players\" or no clear single player, expose the position of the most player-like entity (the thing the human controls).\n181: \n182: ## What the validator checks\n183: \n184: Your output is checked programmatically. No external URLs in `<script src=>`, `<link href=>`, `<img src=>` (regex scan).\n190: 5. Headless Chromium loads the page; no `console.error` or `pageerror` events in 5 seconds.\n191: 6. Canvas has non-blank pixels after 3 seconds (game is actually drawing something).\n192: 7. **Observable contract is present**: `window.__game` exists with the exact shape above, from the first frame.\n193: 8. **Playtest invariants** hold during ~8 seconds of synthetic key input (arrow keys, WASD, space):\n194:    - `state` only takes the values `\"menu\" | \"playing\" | \"gameover\"`.\n195:    - `player.visible` is true for ≥95% of polled frames.\n196:    - Either `score` changes at least once during play, or `state` reaches `\"gameover\"`. let loopId = null;
let lastTime = performance.now();

function startLoop() {
  if (loopId !== null) cancelAnimationFrame(loopId);
  function tick(now) {
    try {
      const dt = Math.min((now - lastTime) / 1000, 0.1);
      lastTime = now;
      // clear canvas
      ctx.clearRect(0,0,canvas.width,canvas.height);
    } catch (err) {
      console.error("loop error", err);
    }
    loopId = requestAnimationFrame(tick);
  }
  loopId = requestAnimationFrame(tick);
}

```js
// === LOOP =============================================================
// Animation loop utilities
let lastTime = performance.now();
let loopId = null;

function startLoop() {
  if (loopId !== null) cancelAnimationFrame(loopId);
  function tick(now) {
    try {
      const dt = Math.min((now - lastTime) / 1000, 0.1);
      lastTime = now;
      // Update logic placeholder
      if (typeof update === "function") update(dt);
      // Render: clear canvas
      ctx.clearRect(0, 0, canvas.width, canvas.height);
      if (typeof render === "function") render();
    } catch (err) {
      console.error("loop error", err);
    }
    loopId = requestAnimationFrame(tick);
  }
  loopId = requestAnimationFrame(tick);
}

// Stub update and render for now
function update(dt) {
  // No game logic yet
}
function render() {
  // Clear canvas (already cleared in loop, but keep here for compatibility)
  ctx.clearRect(0, 0, canvas.width, canvas.height);
}
```

But inside loop we might clear canvas then call render which also clears - duplicates but fine. Or better: only render does clear. So in tick we call update and render only. Let's keep render to clear canvas. So inside tick we call update(dt); render(); inside render, do ctx.clearRect.

Thus loop code:

```
function startLoop() {
  if (loopId !== null) cancelAnimationFrame(loopId);
  function tick(now) {
    try {
      const dt = Math.min((now - lastTime) / 1000, 0.1);
      lastTime = now;
      update(dt);
      render();
    } catch (err) {
      console.error("loop error", err);
    }
    loopId = requestAnimationFrame(tick);
  }
  loopId = requestAnimationFrame(tick);
}
```

Let's implement:

let loopId = null;
let lastTime = performance.now();

function startLoop() {
  if (loopId !== null) cancelAnimationFrame(loopId);
  function tick(now) {
    try {
      const dt = Math.min((now - lastTime) / 1000, 0.1);
      lastTime = now;
      // Update player position based on keys.
      // We'll incorporate movement update directly here or call update().
      const speed = 300; // px/sec as placeholder.
      let dirX = 0, dirY = 0;
      if (keys.has('ArrowRight') || keys.has('d') || keys.has('D')) dirX += 1;
      if (keys.has('ArrowLeft') || keys.has('a') || keys.has('A')) dirX -= 1;
      if (keys.has('ArrowDown') || keys.has('s') || keys.has('S')) dirY += 1;
      if (keys.has('ArrowUp') || keys.has('w') || keys.has('W')) dirY -= 1;
      const len = Math.hypot(dirX, dirY);
      let normX = 0, normY = 0;
      if (len > 0) { normX = dirX / len; normY = dirY / len; }
      player.x += normX * speed * dt;
      player.y += normY * speed * dt;
      // Clamp
      player.x = Math.max(player.radius, Math.min(canvas.width - player.radius, player.x));
      player.y = Math.max(player.radius, Math.min(canvas.height - player.radius, player.y));
      // Clear canvas
      ctx.fillStyle = "#0f1320";
      ctx.fillRect(0, 0, canvas.width, canvas.height);
      // Draw player (simple circle)
      ctx.fillStyle = "#7df9ff";
      ctx.beginPath();
      ctx.arc(player.x, player.y, player.radius, 0, Math.PI * 2);
      ctx.fill();
    } catch (err) {
      console.error("loop error", err);
    }
    loopId = requestAnimationFrame(tick);
  }
  loopId = requestAnimationFrame(tick);
}

Add after line 80 (document.addEventListener('keyup', ...);). Insert the following code:

```js
// === UPDATE ==============================================================
let loopId = null;
let lastTime = performance.now();

function startLoop() {
  if (loopId !== null) cancelAnimationFrame(loopId);
  function tick(now) {
    try {
      const dt = Math.min((now - lastTime) / 1000, 0.1);
      lastTime = now;

      // Update player movement based on keys
      const speed = 300; // pixels per second
      let dirX = (keys.has('ArrowRight') || keys.has('d') || keys.has('D') ? 1 : 0) -
                 (keys.has('ArrowLeft')  || keys.has('a') || keys.has('A') ? 1 : 0);
      let dirY = (keys.has('ArrowDown') || keys.has('s') || keys.has('S') ? 1 : 0) -
                 (keys.has('ArrowUp')   || keys.has('w') || keys.has('W') ? 1 : 0);
      // Normalize diagonal movement
      if (dirX !== 0 && dirY !== 0) {
        const norm = Math.SQRT1_2; // 1/√2
        dirX *= norm;
        dirY *= norm;
      }
      player.x += dirX * speed * dt;
      player.y += dirY * speed * dt;

      // Clamp within canvas bounds (consider current radius)
      player.x = Math.max(player.radius, Math.min(canvas.width - player.radius, player.x));
      player.y = Math.max(player.radius, Math.min(canvas.height - player.radius, player.y));

      // Clear canvas
      ctx.fillStyle = "#0f1320";
      ctx.fillRect(0, 0, canvas.width, canvas.height);

      // Draw player
      ctx.fillStyle = "#7df9ff";
      ctx.beginPath();
      ctx.arc(player.x, player.y, player.radius, 0, Math.PI * 2);
      ctx.fill();

    } catch (err) {
      console.error("loop error", err);
    }
    loopId = requestAnimationFrame(tick);
  }
  loopId = requestAnimationFrame(tick);
}

// === BOOT ==============================================================
startLoop();
```

Insert the following code:\n\n```js\n// === UPDATE ==============================================================\nlet loopId = null;\nlet lastTime = performance.now();\n\nfunction startLoop() {\n  if (loopId !== null) cancelAnimationFrame(loopId);\n  function tick(now) {\n    try {\n      const dt = Math.min((now - lastTime) / 1000, 0.1);\n      lastTime = now;\n\n      // Update player movement based on keys\n      const speed = 300; // pixels per second\n      let dirX = (keys.has('ArrowRight') || keys.has('d') || keys.has('D') ? 1 : 0) -\n                 (keys.has('ArrowLeft')  || keys.has('a') || keys.has('A') ? 1 : 0);\n      let dirY = (keys.has('ArrowDown') || keys.has('s') || keys.has('S') ? 1 : 0) -\n                 (keys.has('ArrowUp')   || keys.has('w') || keys.has('W') ? 1 : 0);\n      // Normalize diagonal movement\n      if (dirX !== 0 && dirY !== 0) {\n        const norm = Math.SQRT1_2; // 1/√2\n        dirX *= norm;\n        dirY *= norm;\n      }\n      player.x += dirX * speed * dt;\n      player.y += dirY * speed * dt;\n\n      // Clamp within canvas bounds (consider current radius)\n      player.x = Math.max(player.radius, Math.min(canvas.width - player.radius, player.x));\n      player.y = Math.max(player.radius, Math.min(canvas.height - player.radius, player.y));\n\n      // Clear canvas\n      ctx.fillStyle = \"#0f1320\";\n      ctx.fillRect(0, 0, canvas.width, canvas.height);\n\n      // Draw player\n      ctx.fillStyle = \"#7df9ff\";\n      ctx.beginPath();\n      ctx.arc(player.x, player.y, player.radius, 0, Math.PI * 2);\n      ctx.fill();\n\n    } catch (err) {\n      console.error(\"loop error\", err);\n    }\n    loopId = requestAnimationFrame(tick);\n  }\n  loopId = requestAnimationFrame(tick);\n}\n\n// === BOOT ==============================================================\nstartLoop();\n```\n\nWe'll insert after line 80 (the 'keyup' listener) maybe with a blank line.\n\nNow, note that we previously defined a variable `keys` in Input block. `player` is defined earlier, `canvas`, `ctx` etc.\n\nPotential errors: We used Math.SQRT1_2, need to be careful about scope. // === UPDATE =============================================================

let spawnTimer = 0; const HAZARD_SPEED = 150; const SPAWN_INTERVAL = 1.5; const SAFE_RADIUS = 200;

function spawnHazard() {
  // spawn at right side moving left
  // Ensure safe distance from player: compute distance from player
  const minX = canvas.width + 20;
  const y = Math.random() * (canvas.height - 40) + 20;
  const dx = minX - player.x;
  const dy = y - player.y;
  if (Math.hypot(dx, dy) < SAFE_RADIUS) {
    // if too close, adjust y to be far
    // we could simply abort spawn for this frame
    return;
  }
  const hazard = {
    x: minX,
    y,
    radius: 12,
    speed: HAZARD_SPEED,
    alive: true,
  };
  hazards.push(hazard);
}

function updateHazards(dt) {
  for (let i = hazards.length - 1; i >= 0; i--) {
    const h = hazards[i];
    h.x -= h.speed * dt;
    if (h.x < -h.radius) {
      hazards.splice(i, 1);
    }
  }
}

function update(dt) {
  // placeholder for other updates (player movement, etc.)
  // Hazard spawn timer
  spawnTimer -= dt;
  if (spawnTimer <= 0) {
    spawnHazard();
    spawnTimer = SPAWN_INTERVAL;
  }
  updateHazards(dt);
}

Then need to call this in startLoop's tick (later). For now we have not defined startLoop, but we will later. It's okay to have update function defined now.

// === HAZARD SYSTEM ==========================================================
const HAZARD_SPEED = 150; // px/sec
const SPAWN_INTERVAL = 1.5; // seconds
const SAFE_RADIUS = 200;

// Timer for spawning hazards
let spawnTimer = 0;

// Define a hazard as an object { x, y, radius, speed, alive }
function spawnHazard() {
  // spawn at right edge, random y
  const y = Math.random() * (canvas.height - 40) + 20;
  const hx = canvas.width + 20;
  // Ensure safe distance from player
  const dx = hx - player.x;
  const dy = y - player.y;
  if (Math.hypot(dx, dy) < SAFE_RADIUS) {
    // Too close, skip this spawn
    return;
  }
  hazards.push({ x: hx, y, radius: 12, speed: HAZARD_SPEED, alive: true });
}

function updateHazards(dt) {
  for (let i = hazards.length - 1; i >= 0; i--) {
    const h = hazards[i];
    h.x -= h.speed * dt;
    if (h.x < -h.radius) {
      hazards.splice(i, 1);
    }
  }
}

// Main update function placeholder (called each frame by startLoop)
function update(dt) {
  // Hazard spawning
  spawnTimer -= dt;
  if (spawnTimer <= 0) {
    spawnHazard();
    spawnTimer = SPAWN_INTERVAL;
  }
  // Update existing hazards
  updateHazards(dt);
}
  
  </script>
```

function update(dt) {
  // spawn logic...
  // update hazards...
  // collision detection
  if (state === "playing") {
    for (const h of hazards) {
      const dx = h.x - player.x;
      const dy = h.y - player.y;
      const distSq = dx*dx + dy*dy;
      const radSum = h.radius + player.radius;
      if (distSq < radSum*radSum) {
        state = "gameover";
        // optional break
        break;
      }
    }
  }
}

We also need to ensure that update is called somewhere; currently no loop but will be later. Adding this should be fine.

Define beep function:

```
let audioCtx = null;
function beep(freq, durSec) {
  try {
    if (!audioCtx) audioCtx = new (window.AudioContext || window.webkitAudioContext)();
    const osc = audioCtx.createOscillator();
    const gain = audioCtx.createGain();
    osc.type = "sine";
    osc.frequency.value = freq;
    gain.gain.setValueAtTime(0.05, audioCtx.currentTime);
    gain.gain.exponentialRampToValueAtTime(0.001, audioCtx.currentTime + durSec);
    osc.connect(gain);
    gain.connect(audioCtx.destination);
    osc.start();
    osc.stop(audioCtx.currentTime + durSec);
  } catch (e) {
    // swallow audio errors
  }
}
```

Then we need to trigger beep on events: "hit, score, and death". We have collision detection when player collides with hazard: set state "gameover". We can call beep(110, 0.25) for death, in the collision detection block.

prompt (5,162 chars)
Read CONVENTIONS.md and DESIGN.md in your working directory before doing anything else.

Your job in this stage is to break the implementation into a numbered list of small, ordered tasks. The next stage will work through this list one task at a time, in a single LLM session, reloading the page after each task and capturing console errors for you to fix.

Title: Size Shift
Concept: Tap to toggle the dot between a tiny and a large radius; the small size threads through narrow gaps while the large size can smash weak barriers or reach distant platforms, demanding rapid size changes.

**You must produce one output file: `TASKS.md` in the working directory.** Use your write/edit tool to create this file on disk. After your turn ends, the validator runs `readFile("TASKS.md")` — if the file does not exist on disk, validation fails and your work is wasted. **Do NOT reply with the task list as inline chat text.** The list must be written to the file.

**Tool calls MUST be emitted as actual function/tool invocations — NOT described or simulated inside your reasoning/analysis text.** Writing tool-shaped JSON like `{"filePath":"TASKS.md","content":"..."}` *inside your chain-of-thought* does NOT invoke any tool. It is just text in the analysis channel that nothing reads or executes. The same goes for `{"pattern":"**/*"}` etc. — those only do something when emitted as a real `tool_use` event from the action channel, never when written into your thinking. Several prior runs have failed by hallucinating tool calls inside reasoning; do not repeat that pattern. If at any point you "describe" a tool call in your thinking instead of invoking it, you must immediately invoke the real tool afterward. The format MUST be a numbered list, one task per line, each line starting with `<number>.`:

```
1. <Task title> — <one-sentence description of what to do>
2. <Task title> — <one-sentence description>
...
```

The next stage parses tasks by matching `^\d+\.` at the start of each line. Don't use bullet sub-lists, don't number sub-tasks (1.1, 1.2). One number, one task, one line.

### What makes a good task

- **Small enough** to complete in a single coding turn (~50–150 lines of edits).
- **Testable**: after this task, what does the page do that it didn't before?
- **Leaves the file runnable**: at the end of the task, no syntax errors, no uncaught exceptions on load. Even if the game isn't playable yet, it must load.
- **Specific**: don't say "implement player". Say "implement player movement: arrow keys set keys.left/right; in update(), apply acceleration in the pressed direction with cap at MAX_SPEED, friction otherwise; clamp x to canvas bounds."
- **Concrete numbers**: this is where pixel sizes, ms intervals, and px/sec speeds live. Pick them. The implement stage will use them verbatim.

### Required coverage (in roughly this order)

Cover at minimum, ordered as below. Add tasks if the design demands more (powerups, level progression, screen wrap, etc):

1. **HTML scaffold + meta.json**: header (h1, description, controls list), canvas, restart button per CONVENTIONS.md. Stub `<script>` block (empty). meta.json matching header text.
2. **Game state objects**: player, hazards, score, phase using plain object literals at the top of the script. Set initial values.
3. **requestAnimationFrame loop**: startLoop() with `cancelAnimationFrame(loopId)` discipline at the top. Body wrapped in try/catch. Calls update(dt) and render(). Don't draw anything yet besides clearing the canvas.
4. **Input**: a `keys` Set populated by document keydown/keyup. R-key restart hook. Restart button click hook. Both call the same reset() function.
5. **Player movement**: dt-based physics tied to the key inputs from task 4. Verify each control listed in meta.json actually changes a value.
6. **Hazards**: data structure, spawn rules with a SAFE_RADIUS so nothing spawns on top of the player at t=0, motion (dt-scaled).
7. **Collision detection**: player vs hazards. On hit, transition phase to "gameover" and stop the active gameplay.
8. **Score**: discrete events that increase score, drawn on canvas at top-left.
9. **Render**: clear canvas, draw background, hazards, player, HUD. Player must be the highest-contrast object on screen.
10. **Game-over overlay**: dim background, "Game Over", final score, "Press R or click Restart".
11. **Observable contract**: populate `window.__game = { state, score, player:{x,y,visible}, objective }` every frame. `state` is "playing" | "gameover". `objective` is a 10–200 char single-sentence description.
12. **Audio**: lazy-init AudioContext inside a beep() helper, wrapped in try/catch. Hook into hit / score / death events.

You may merge two trivial tasks if they truly belong together (e.g. tasks 1+2 if state is just three lines). You may split a complex task in two. Aim for 10–15 tasks total.

**Final check before you stop**: confirm you actually called your write/edit tool with `TASKS.md` as the path. If you only described the task list in chat without invoking the tool, the file will not exist on disk and validation will fail. After the file is written, stop. Do not begin implementation.
